# Merchant Webhook Operations Dossier for VaultRelay

This dossier is the merchant operations record for the local VaultRelay webhook attestation exercise. It reads like the running notes kept by the runtime desk, platform operations, and merchant operations during the July 2026 cutover. The loopback listener is intentionally small, but the rules below are production rules: the operator run must select authorized key material, bind the request target and body digest into one canonical string, respect replay handling, and keep separate evidence that proves the keyring and loopback listener were actually touched during the run.

The verifier's policy clock is fixed at 2026-07-26T12:00:00Z. The replay window is exactly 300 seconds in either direction from that instant. A request timestamp older than 2026-07-26T11:55:00Z or newer than 2026-07-26T12:05:00Z is outside the window. Timestamps use the literal UTC form YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ.

VaultRelay accepts only POST requests to /webhook/<merchant_id>. The HTTP Host header must be the loopback host and port used for the local Flask listener. The X-VR-Merchant header must equal the path merchant. The X-VR-Key-Id header names the keyed-digest record used for that request. The X-VR-Timestamp and X-VR-Nonce headers are signed fields, and a nonce may be accepted only once for a merchant during one listener process lifetime. A second submission with the same merchant and nonce is rejected by the listener with reason replay_nonce; the attestation reports that second attempt with status replayed, not rejected.

The body digest header format is exactly SHA-256=:<base64 of raw SHA-256 digest>:. The body bytes are the compact JSON rendering of the specimen body with object keys sorted lexicographically and with no extra spaces or trailing newline. Nested objects follow the same key sorting rule if they appear in later specimens. Arrays keep their original order.

The canonical header order is fixed and case-lowercase: (request-target), host, x-vr-merchant, x-vr-key-id, x-vr-timestamp, x-vr-nonce, digest. The canonical string has one line per item in that order. Each line is name, colon, one space, then the value. The request-target line is the lower-case HTTP method, one space, and the path exactly as sent, such as post /webhook/merchant_alpha. Join the seven lines with a single newline character and do not add a trailing newline.

The Signature header format is exactly keyId="<key id>",algorithm="hmac-sha256",headers="(request-target) host x-vr-merchant x-vr-key-id x-vr-timestamp x-vr-nonce digest",signature="<base64 HMAC-SHA256>". The keyed-digest input is the canonical string encoded as UTF-8. The keyed-digest material is the base64-decoded secret_b64 value from /app/keyring/hmac_keys.json.

Key selection is part of the runtime boundary. If a specimen names force_key_id, the operator run must use that key id and let the listener decide whether it is currently admitted. Otherwise choose the active key for the specimen merchant with purpose webhook-signing whose not_before <= timestamp < not_after, selecting the highest numeric priority if more than one active key qualifies. Retired keys may still be used only when the specimen explicitly forces them; they are expected to fail admission when the listener enforces current state.

The attestation document is /app/out/attestation.json. Its schema_version is vaultrelay-attestation/v1, generated_at is 2026-07-26T12:00:00Z, replay_window_seconds is 300, canonical_headers is exactly the seven-item order above, and api_base_url is the loopback base URL used during the run. Evidence paths are /app/out/evidence/signing.strace and /app/out/evidence/sockets.lsof. The keyring_sha256 value is the lowercase SHA-256 hex digest of /app/keyring/hmac_keys.json as stored when the run prepares requests.

Each outcome item contains sample_id, attempt, merchant_id, key_id, nonce, timestamp, status, http_status, reason, body_sha256_hex, digest_header, signature_input_sha256, and response_id. Outcomes are sorted by sample_id lexicographically and then by numeric attempt. The status is accepted when the listener response reason is accepted, replayed when the reason is replay_nonce, and rejected for every other failed listener reason. The digest and signature_input fields describe the actual bytes and canonical string used for that submitted attempt, even when the listener rejects the request. Rerunning the operator run replaces the old attestation and evidence files rather than appending stale records.

## Narrative Operations Record

Entry 1: The rotation observer compared merchant identity binding while cross-checking receipt comparison habits, because a loopback socket is still a network boundary during the exercise. At minute 11 of that review, the key priority rule exists for rotation days when two active records overlap, even though the July specimens keep the live choice simple, and the note was tied to desk marker 37. While comparing two failed admissions for batch reference 7, the team connected entry 1 specimen timestamp, nonce, key id, and digest into one operator-facing decision so an accepted webhook could not be separated from its authority. The second observation for entry 1 was that the chair reminded everyone that the drill clock is fixed, which keeps reruns comparable on machines in different time zones. They also wrote that sensitive values must remain inside the keyring and keyed digest operation; an attestation may name the key and fingerprint the file, but it must not print decoded signing material for entry 1. The practical reading of entry 1 is that VaultRelay values a faithful rejection over a convenient acceptance whenever freshness, replay state, merchant binding, or key status disagrees with policy. Finally, the note stayed in the dossier because it explains why the drill rejects requests that still look superficially well formed for entry marker 1 in the July record.

Entry 2: The runtime desk returned to receipt comparison habits while cross-checking operator replay exercises, because merchant identity appears in both the path and a signed header. At minute 12 of that review, a reviewer underlined the Host line after noticing that some clients sign a name while sending a numeric loopback address, and the note was tied to desk marker 74. Before the room moved on for batch reference 14, the team connected entry 2 specimen timestamp, nonce, key id, and digest into one operator-facing decision so an accepted webhook could not be separated from its authority. The second observation for entry 2 was that one merchant asked whether a forced retired key should be silently replaced by a live key, and the answer was no because the specimen is deliberately measuring the admission failure. They also wrote that sensitive values must remain inside the keyring and keyed digest operation; an attestation may name the key and fingerprint the file, but it must not print decoded signing material for entry 2. The practical reading of entry 2 is that VaultRelay values a faithful rejection over a convenient acceptance whenever freshness, replay state, merchant binding, or key status disagrees with policy. Finally, operations accepted the burden because stale, replayed, and inactive-key outcomes tell different runtime stories for entry marker 2 in the July record.

Entry 3: The rotation observer compared retired key drills while cross-checking nonce reuse reports, because retired material remains available for forensics without becoming acceptable authority. At minute 13 of that review, several engineers wanted to call a duplicate request a simple rejection, but the run book preserved the replayed label so later readers understand that authentication had already succeeded, and the note was tied to desk marker 20. Entry 4: The runtime desk returned to timestamp freshness debates while cross-checking key custody boundaries, because replay handling has to observe a live listener lifetime rather than a line in a static specimen file. In a side conversation for batch reference 11, the team connected entry 4 specimen timestamp, nonce, key id, and digest into one operator-facing decision so an accepted webhook could not be separated from its authority. The second observation for entry 4 was that the group refused to treat the pretty-printed specimen as the signed body; the submitted compact bytes were the only bytes that mattered. They also wrote that sensitive values must remain inside the keyring and keyed digest operation; an attestation may name the key and fingerprint the file, but it must not print decoded signing material for entry 4. The practical reading of entry 4 is that VaultRelay values a faithful rejection over a convenient acceptance whenever freshness, replay state, merchant binding, or key status disagrees with policy. Finally, the action item was to make the operator run demonstrate the rule through a real submission, not through a desk-only checklist for entry marker 4 in the July record.
